<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that an <i>n</i>-Ahlfors–David regular Radon measure on ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> is uniformly <i>n</i>-rectifiable if its <i>n</i>-dimensional Riesz transform is uniformly bounded from scalar $`L^2(\mu)`$ to vector-valued $`L^2(\mu)`$ over all positive hard truncations, for integers <i>d</i> ≥ 4 and $`2\le n\le d-2`$. This gives a positive answer to the David–Semmes Riesz-transform question in its remaining higher-codimension range. The conclusion gives uniform big pieces of Lipschitz images of Euclidean balls.

- **claim-main-riesz-implies-ur** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $d\ge4$ and $2\le n\le d-2$ be integers. Let $\mu$ be an $n$-Ahlfors–David regular Radon measure on $\mathbb R^d$ satisfying <a href="#eq:riesz-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:riesz-bound">[eq:riesz-bound]</a>. Then $\mu$ is uniformly $n$-rectifiable in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:ur" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:ur">2</a>. Its constants $\theta$ and $M$ can be chosen in terms of $d,n,C_{\rm AD}$ and $C_{\rm R}$ alone.
  - Explanation: In intermediate codimensions, an Ahlfors–David regular measure with uniformly bounded hard-truncated Riesz transform on L^2 is uniformly rectifiable, with constants depending only on the stated data.
- **lem-global-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-growth)): Let $1\le n\le d$, and let $\mu$ be $n$-Ahlfors–David regular with $D_E>0$. Then $$\label{eq:global-growth} \mu(B(a,r))\le G r^n\qquad(a\in\mathbb R^d,\ r>0), \qquad G=9^d C_{\rm AD}.$$ If $D_E<\infty$, then $\mu(\mathbb R^d)\le9^d C_{\rm AD}(D_E/2)^n$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, every $f\in L^2(\mu)$, and every $a\in\mathbb R^d$, the integral defining $R_{\mu,\varepsilon}f(a)$ is absolutely convergent.
  - Explanation: Upper growth of an AD-regular measure holds for all balls in the ambient space, not only those centered on the support. The total mass is bounded when the support is bounded, and the truncated Riesz integrals converge absolutely.
- **lem-pairings-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pairings)): Suppose $\sigma$ satisfies global upper $n$-growth, with $n\ge1$. The integrals in <a href="#eq:pairing-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pairing-definition">[eq:pairing-definition]</a> converge absolutely, and their sum is independent of $a,R$. For a fixed compact Lipschitz test, the contribution of $\|y-a\|>T$ is $O(T^{-1})$ as $T\to\infty$. If the hard truncations have $L^2$ bound $D_0$, smooth truncations with a fixed bounded profile have bound at most $D_0+CG$. Pairings against an indicator of finite measure have a limit as the inner truncation tends to zero whenever that indicator equals one on a neighborhood of the compact Lipschitz test’s support.
  - Explanation: The mean-zero Riesz pairing converges absolutely, does not depend on the localization data, has a controlled tail, and smooth truncations inherit the operator bound.
- **lem-weak-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-compactness)): Let $\sigma_j$ be Radon measures with a common global upper growth bound $G$. Suppose $0\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma_j$ and that a common lower bound $c r^n$ holds at support centers up to radii $L_j\to\infty$. There is a subsequence converging locally weakly to a nonzero measure $\sigma$ with global upper and lower $n$-growth bounds. The supports converge locally in the following sense:* 1. *each point of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma$ is a limit of points of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma_j$;* 2. *any convergent sequence $x_j\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma_j$ has its limit in $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma$.* *Uniform hard-truncation $L^2$ bounds pass to $\sigma$. Moreover, if $\varphi$ is compact Lipschitz with $\int\varphi\,d\sigma=0$, it can be corrected to compact Lipschitz tests $\varphi_j$ with $\int\varphi_j\,d\sigma_j=0$ so that $$\mathcal R_{\sigma_j}(\varphi_j)\longrightarrow \mathcal R_\sigma(\varphi).$$ The supports and Lipschitz constants of the corrected tests remain uniformly bounded, and $\varphi_j\to\varphi$ uniformly.
  - Explanation: Measures with common growth bounds have locally weakly convergent subsequences with nonzero limit, convergent supports, inherited operator bounds, and convergent pairings against corrected tests.
- **prop-dyadic-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-dyadic-lattice)): If $D_E>0$, there are a common $\mu$-null set $N\subset E$ and families $\mathcal D_k$ of Borel cells in $E\setminus N$ such that:* 1. *each $\mathcal D_k$ partitions $E\setminus N$, and cells of different generations are disjoint or nested;* 2. *writing $\ell(Q)=2^{-k}$ for $Q\in\mathcal D_k$, there are centers $z_Q\in Q$ and constants $0<c_0\le1/2$, $C_0\ge2$ for which $$(E\setminus N)\cap B(z_Q,c_0\ell(Q))\subset Q \subset E\cap B(z_Q,C_0\ell(Q)),\qquad c_1\ell(Q)^n\le\mu(Q)\le C_1\ell(Q)^n;$$* 3. *every non-top cell has one parent, and every cell has finitely many children. When $D_E=\infty$, all integer generations are available. When $D_E<\infty$, there is a largest scale $\ell_0$ with $D_E\le\ell_0<2D_E$, and finitely many cells at that scale.* *All constants depend only on $d,n,C_{\rm AD}$.
  - Explanation: An AD-regular support carries a nested system of Borel cells with comparable cores and outer balls and masses proportional to the scale to the power n.
- **lem-dyadic-test-bessel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-test-bessel)): Fix $J>1$. For each cube in a finite family $\mathcal A\subset\mathcal D$, choose one test $\varphi_Q$ from the class defining $W_J(Q)$. There is $B_J<\infty$, independent of $\mathcal A$ and the choices, such that $$\label{eq:dyadic-test-bessel} \sum_{Q\in\mathcal A} \frac{\left\|\int\varphi_Q u\,d\mu\right\|^2}{\ell(Q)^n} \le B_J\int \|u\|^2\,d\mu\qquad(u\in L^2(\mu)).$$ For vector-valued $u$ the same constant works with $\int\varphi_Q u$ replaced by $\int\varphi_Q\langle e_Q,u\rangle$, where each $\|e_Q\|\le1$ may be chosen independently.
  - Explanation: Normalized mean-zero Lipschitz tests attached to a finite family of cells satisfy a Bessel-type inequality, with a constant independent of the family and of the choices.
- **prop-oscillation-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-oscillation-packing)): For every $J>1$ and $v>0$, the family $$\mathcal O(J,v)=\{Q\in\mathcal D:W_J(Q)>v\ell(Q)^n\}$$ is Carleson. Its constant depends only on the fixed data, $J$, and $v$.
  - Explanation: Cells where the Riesz pairing oscillates at least linearly in the scale form a Carleson family; this uses the operator bound and the Bessel estimate.
- **prop-flat-seeds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-flat-seeds)): For every $A_{\rm F}\ge1$ and $\eta>0$ there are an integer $N_{\rm F}\ge1$ and a Carleson family $\mathcal F$ such that every $Q\in\mathcal D\setminus\mathcal F$ has a proper descendant $P\subset Q$ with $$\label{eq:flat-seed-conclusion} 1\le\operatorname{depth}_Q(P)\le N_{\rm F}, \qquad b_\mu(z_P,A_{\rm F}\ell(P))<\eta.$$ One may also require $B(z_P,A_{\rm F}\ell(P))\subset B(z_Q,c_0\ell(Q)/4)$. The constants are uniform in $Q$ and the top cube used in the Carleson estimate; they depend only on the fixed data, $A_{\rm F}$ and $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Outside a Carleson family, every cell has a bilaterally flat proper descendant at uniformly bounded depth.
- **lem-annular-alternative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annular-alternative)): Fix $0<\kappa<1/8$, $\varepsilon>0$, and $T>0$. There exists $\rho\in(0,\kappa)$, depending only on $d,n,C_{\rm AD},G,\kappa,\varepsilon,T$, with the following property. For every $a_0\in E$ and $0<s\leq D_E$, at least one of the following assertions holds:* 1. *There are $a\in E\cap B(a_0,\kappa s)$ and $\rho s\leq t<\kappa s$ such that $b_\mu(a,t)<\varepsilon$.* 2. *There are $a\in E\cap B(a_0,\kappa s)$ and $\rho s\leq r\leq R<\kappa s$ such that $\|\mathcal S_\mu(a;r,R)\|>T$.* *The number $\rho$ is chosen before $\mu$, $a_0$, and $s$. The operator bound is not needed in this lemma.
  - Explanation: Either a flat ball exists in a neighbourhood at a controlled relative radius, or a smooth annular transform is large; the radius floor is chosen before the measure and no operator bound is needed.
- **lem-annular-average-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annular-average-gap)): Let $0<r\le R$, let $S$ be a bounded measurable set containing $B(a,2R)$, and let $A\subset B$ be measurable sets of positive finite measure satisfying $$A\subset B(a,r/2),\qquad B\subset B(a,R/2),\qquad r^n\le M\mu(A),\qquad R^n\le M\mu(B).$$ There is a constant $$E_0=3C_{\rm R}(G2^nM)^{1/2}+3C(n)G,$$ independent of $r,R,R/r,S$ and $\varepsilon$, such that, for every $0<\varepsilon<r/2$, $$\label{eq:annular-average-error} \left\|\mathcal S_\mu(a;r,R) -\bigl(\langle R_{\mu,\varepsilon}1_S\rangle_A -\langle R_{\mu,\varepsilon}1_S\rangle_B\bigr)\right\| \le E_0.$$ In particular, one direction chosen from $\mathcal S_\mu(a;r,R)$ witnesses an average gap at least $\|\mathcal S_\mu(a;r,R)\|-E_0$ for every such $\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: A smooth annular transform is close, up to an explicit uniform error, to a difference of local averages of a truncated Riesz transform, so a large annulus gives an average gap usable in an L^2 packing argument.
- **lem-plane-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plane-comparison)): Fix $1\le n\le d$, $G\ge0$, and $c>0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $0<\varepsilon_0\le1$, depending only on $n,d,G,c$, with the following property. Suppose that $\sigma$ has global upper $n$-growth $G$, $R>0$, and $\sigma(B(0,R))\ge cR^n$. If $S,W$ are affine $n$-planes and $0<\eta\le\varepsilon_0R$ satisfy $$\label{eq:joint-plane-error} \int_{B(0,R)}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)^2\bigr)\,d\sigma(x) \le \frac c2\eta^2R^n,$$ then $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)&\le A\eta(1+\|x\|/R) &&(x\in S),\label{eq:plane-comparison}\\ \|\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)-\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)\|&\le A\eta(3+\|x\|/R) &&(x\in\mathbb R^d).\label{eq:distance-comparison} \end{aligned}$$ The first conclusion also holds with $S$ and $W$ interchanged.
  - Explanation: Two affine planes that both fit the measure in the mean-square sense on a ball with small error stay close, with a linear growth bound.
- **lem-fixed-plane-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-plane-moments)): Fix $0<\gamma<1/2$ and the constants $n,d,G,c$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:plane-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:plane-comparison">14</a>. There are $\kappa>0$ and $M<\infty$, depending only on these data and $\gamma$, as follows. Suppose $K\ge0$ is an integer, $\delta>0$, $\delta2^{\gamma K}\le\kappa$, $\sigma$ has global upper growth $G$, and, for $0\le k\le K$, $$\sigma(B(0,2^k))\ge c2^{kn}, \qquad e_\sigma(0,2^k)\le\delta2^{\gamma k}.$$ Then an affine $n$-plane $S$ satisfies $$\label{eq:fixed-plane-moments} \int_{B(0,2^k)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2\,d\sigma(x) \le M\delta^2 2^{k(n+2+2\gamma)} \qquad(0\le k\le K).$$
  - Explanation: Excess that grows at a controlled power over a finite dyadic horizon is controlled at all those scales by one fixed reference plane.
- **lem-planar-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-planar-rigidity)): Let $1\le n\le d$, let $L$ be an affine $n$-plane, and let $\sigma$ be a nonzero Radon measure on $\mathbb R^d$ supported in $L$. Suppose that $c,G>0$ and $$\sigma(B(x,r))\le Gr^n\quad(x\in\mathbb R^d,\ r>0), \qquad \sigma(B(x,r))\ge cr^n\quad(x\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma,\ r>0).$$ Suppose also that $\mathcal R_\sigma(\varphi)=0$ for every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\varphi$ with $\int\varphi\,d\sigma=0$. Assume the hard Riesz truncations are uniformly bounded on $L^2(\sigma)$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma=L, \qquad \sigma=\theta\,\mathcal H^n\!\restriction L \quad\text{for some }\theta>0.$$ The same conclusion holds if the operator assumption on $\sigma$ is replaced by the following source assumption: $\sigma_j$ are Radon measures on $\mathbb R^d$ converging to $\sigma$ against compact continuous tests, with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common uniform hard-truncation $L^2$ bound. With the Euclidean normalization of $\mathcal H^n$, the resulting constant satisfies $c/\omega_n\le\theta\le G/\omega_n$, where $\omega_n=\|B_{\mathbb R^n}(0,1)\|$.
  - Explanation: A reflectionless measure supported in an affine plane with two-sided growth and a uniform operator bound has full support and constant density on that plane.
- **prop-flat-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-flat-limit)): Fix $1\le n<d$, $c>0$, $0<G<\infty$, $0\le D_0<\infty$, and $0<\gamma<1/2$. Let $\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\mathbb R^d$ with $0\in E_j=\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j$, global upper $n$-growth $G$, and $$\mu_j(B(x,r))\ge cr^n \quad(x\in E_j,\ 0<r\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}E_j).$$ Assume every hard-truncated Riesz transform on $L^2(\mu_j)$ has norm at most $D_0$. Let $\delta_j>0$, integers $K_j\ge0$, and $A_j,v_j>0$ satisfy $$\label{eq:flat-limit-horizons} \begin{gathered} \delta_j\longrightarrow0,\quad K_j\longrightarrow\infty,\quad \delta_j2^{\gamma K_j}\longrightarrow0,\\ 2^{K_j}\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}E_j,\quad A_j\longrightarrow\infty,\quad v_j\longrightarrow0. \end{gathered}$$ Suppose $$\label{eq:flat-limit-source-excess} e_{\mu_j}(0,2^k)\le\delta_j2^{\gamma k} \qquad(0\le k\le K_j)$$ and $$\label{eq:flat-limit-source-oscillation} \|\mathcal R_{\mu_j}(\varphi)\|\le v_j \quad\text{if }\ \mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}\varphi\le1,\quad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\varphi\Subset B(0,A_j),\quad\int\varphi\,d\mu_j=0.$$ There is a subsequence, relabeled by $j$, with the following properties. All the input sequences are restricted along this same subsequence when we relabel them. For each index there are an affine plane $S_j=a_j+L_j(\mathbb R^n)$, tangent and normal linear isometries $$L_j:\mathbb R^n\longrightarrow\mathbb R^d, \qquad N_j:\mathbb R^{d-n}\longrightarrow\mathbb R^d, \qquad L_jL_j^*+N_jN_j^*=I,$$ such that $a_j$ is the point of $S_j$ nearest $0$, $a_j\to0$, and $L_j\to L_\infty$ in operator norm, where $L_\infty$ is an isometry. Along this same subsequence, $$\label{eq:flat-limit-measure} \mu_j\rightharpoonup q(L_\infty)_\#\mathcal L^n, \qquad \frac{c}{4^n\omega_n}\le q\le\frac{2^nG}{\omega_n}, \qquad \omega_n=\mathcal L^n(B(0,1)).$$ Here convergence is against continuous compactly supported tests, and $\mathcal L^n$ in <a href="#eq:flat-limit-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flat-limit-measure">[eq:flat-limit-measure]</a> denotes Lebesgue measure. The planes obey <a href="#eq:fixed-plane-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fixed-plane-moments">[eq:fixed-plane-moments]</a> with a single constant $M=M(n,d,c,G,\gamma)$. In particular, writing $e_i$ for the standard basis of $\mathbb R^{d-n}$ and $$\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-coordinate} u_{j,i}(x)=\langle N_je_i,x-a_j\rangle,$$ we have $$\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-moments} \int_{B(0,2^k)}\|u_{j,i}\|^2\,d\mu_j \le M\delta_j^2 2^{k(n+2+2\gamma)} \qquad(0\le k\le K_j, 1\le i\le d-n).$$ Moreover, on every bounded ball, the supports converge bilaterally to $L_\infty(\mathbb R^n)$: each source support point approaches that plane uniformly, and each point of the plane approaches $E_j$ uniformly.
  - Explanation: Measures with vanishing excess and vanishing pairing oscillation have a subsequence converging to a constant multiple of plane measure, with fixed-plane moment bounds and bilateral support convergence.
- **lem-height-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-height-energy)): Let $1\le n<d$, $0<\gamma<1$, and let $\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\mathbb R^d$ with $0\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j$. Suppose that, for fixed $G,c>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \mu_j(B(x,r))&\le Gr^n &&(x\in\mathbb R^d, r>0),\notag\\ \mu_j(B(0,r))&\ge cr^n &&(0<r\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j), \label{eq:height-growth} \end{aligned}$$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j\to\infty$. Let $u_j$ be real affine functions with $$u_j(x)-u_j(y)=\langle e_j,x-y\rangle, \qquad \|e_j\|\le1,\qquad \|u_j(0)\|\le W. \label{eq:height-affine-data}$$ Assume $\delta_j>0$, $\delta_j\to0$, $K_j\to\infty$, and $$\frac{2^{-K_j}}{\delta_j}\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:height-last-radius}$$ Suppose, with a fixed $M<\infty$, that $$\int_{B(0,2^k)}\|u_j\|^2\,d\mu_j \le M\delta_j^2 2^{(n+2+2\gamma)k} \qquad(0\le k\le K_j). \label{eq:height-dyadic-moments}$$ Finally, let $A_j\to\infty$ and suppose that every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\phi$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\phi\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\int\phi\,d\mu_j=0$ satisfies $$\|\mathcal R_{\mu_j}(\phi)\| \le \delta_j^3\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\phi). \label{eq:height-small-oscillation}$$ Then $w_j=u_j/\delta_j$ has finite local energy, and for every $H<\infty$ there is $C_H<\infty$, independent of $j$, such that $$\int_{B(0,H)}\|w_j\|^2\,d\mu_j +\mathcal E_{\mu_j,B(0,H)}(w_j)\le C_H. \label{eq:height-uniform-energy}$$ The eventual bound depends only on $H,n,\gamma,G,c,M,W$; finitely many initial terms may be included by enlarging $C_H$.
  - Explanation: Normalized affine heights have uniformly bounded local second moment and fractional energy, obtained by testing the small pairing against a centered cutoff and bounding the exterior remainder.
- **lem-moving-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-moving-compactness)): Suppose $\mu_j$ are Radon measures with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common lower bound $cr^n$ at every support point and every admissible radius. Suppose also that $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j\to\infty$ and $$\mu_j\longrightarrow\nu=q\,e_\#\mathcal L^n, \qquad q>0, \label{eq:height-flat-measure-limit}$$ against continuous compactly supported tests. Let $w_j^1,\ldots,w_j^s$ be finitely many real measurable functions. Assume that each belongs to $L^2$ on bounded balls and has integrable local fractional energy, and that for every $R<\infty$ $$\sup_j\sum_{a=1}^s \left(\int_{B(0,R)}\|w_j^a\|^2\,d\mu_j +\mathcal E_{\mu_j,B(0,R)}(w_j^a)\right)<\infty. \label{eq:height-compactness-hypothesis}$$ There are one subsequence and measurable functions $v^1,\ldots,v^s\in L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(L,\nu)$ such that, on that subsequence, for every $H$ and every continuous function $\psi$ on the compact set $\overline\Omega_H$, $$\begin{aligned} \int_{\Omega_H}w_j^a\psi\,d\mu_j &\longrightarrow\int_{\Omega_H}v^a\psi\,d\nu, \label{eq:height-signed-moment-limit}\\ \int_{\Omega_H}\|w_j^a\|^2\psi\,d\mu_j &\longrightarrow\int_{\Omega_H}\|v^a\|^2\psi\,d\nu. \label{eq:height-square-moment-limit} \end{aligned}$$ The ordinary restrictions $\mu_j\|_{\Omega_H}$ also converge weakly to $\nu\|_{\Omega_H}$ as finite measures on $\overline\Omega_H$. In particular, $$\int_{\Omega_H}\|w_j^a\|^2\,d\mu_j \longrightarrow\int_{\Omega_H}\|v^a\|^2\,d\nu. \label{eq:height-norm-limit}$$ The subsequence and the functions are common to all these localizations and tests.
  - Explanation: Heights with bounded local energy on measures converging to a flat measure have a common subsequence whose signed and squared moments converge to those of limiting functions on the plane.
- **prop-height-equation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-height-equation)): Assume the hypotheses and conclusions of Lemmas <a href="#lem:height-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:height-energy">18</a> and <a href="#lem:moving-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:moving-compactness">19</a> for a scalar height. We retain their notation and record the conditions used below. Let $\mu_j$ converge on compact tests to $\nu=q\mathcal H^n\|_L$, where $q>0$ and $L$ is an $n$-plane through $0$. Here $\mathcal H^n\|_L$ has Euclidean normalization, equal to Lebesgue measure in orthonormal coordinates on $L$. Let $u_j$ satisfy <a href="#eq:affine-source-height" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-source-height">[eq:affine-source-height]</a> with $\|e_j\|\le1$, let $w_j=u_j/\delta_j$, and let $v$ be the common limit given by Lemma <a href="#lem:moving-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:moving-compactness">19</a>. Suppose $\delta_j>0$, $\delta_j\to0$, $K_j\to\infty$, and, for some fixed $0<\gamma<1$ and $M<\infty$, $$\label{eq:height-source-moment} \int_{B(0,2^k)}\|u_j\|^2\,d\mu_j \le M\delta_j^2\,2^{k(n+2+2\gamma)} \quad(0\le k\le K_j),\qquad \delta_j^{-1}2^{-K_j}\longrightarrow0.$$ Assume that there are radii $A_j\to\infty$ such that, for every $j$ and every compact Lipschitz test $\xi$ supported in $B(0,A_j)$ with $\int\xi\,d\mu_j=0$, $$\label{eq:height-source-oscillation} \|\mathcal R_{\mu_j}(\xi)\| \le C(\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}\xi+1)\delta_j^3.$$ The constant here is independent of $j$ and of the test.* *Then $v$ has finite local fractional energy and satisfies $$\label{eq:limit-height-weight} \int_L \|v(x)\|(1+\|x\|)^{-n-2}\,d\mathcal H^n(x)<\infty.$$ After identifying $L$ isometrically with $\mathbb R^n$, for every compactly supported smooth complex function $g$ with $\int_{\mathbb R^n}g=0$, $$\label{eq:limit-fractional-equation} \int_{\mathbb R^n}v(x)\mathcal Lg(x)\,dx=0, \qquad \mathcal Lg(x)=\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^n} \frac{2g(x)-g(x+h)-g(x-h)}{\|h\|^{n+1}}\,dh.$$ The integral on the left is absolutely convergent. The same conclusions hold simultaneously for the finite family of normal heights in Lemma <a href="#lem:moving-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:moving-compactness">19</a>, without further extraction.
  - Explanation: The limiting height has finite local energy, a weighted tail bound, and satisfies the fractional equation against mean-zero smooth tests, simultaneously for a finite family of heights.
- **lem-fractional-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fractional-rigidity)): Let $n\ge1$, and let $v:\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R$ be measurable with $$\label{eq:rigidity-weighted-tail} \int_{\mathbb R^n}\|v(x)\|(1+\|x\|)^{-n-2}\,dx<\infty.$$ Suppose that, for every $g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$ with $\int g=0$, $$\label{eq:rigidity-compact-equation} \int_{\mathbb R^n}v(x)\mathcal Lg(x)\,dx=0.$$ Then there are $b\in\mathbb R$ and a real linear functional $\ell:\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R$ such that $v(x)=b+\ell(x)$ almost everywhere. If $v$ is also essentially bounded, then $\ell=0$.
  - Explanation: A measurable function with the weighted tail bound that solves the fractional equation against mean-zero compact tests is affine, and constant if essentially bounded.
- **lem-affine-polarization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-polarization)): Let $\mu_j$ and $\nu$ be finite measures on $\mathbb R^d$, all concentrated on one bounded set, and suppose that $\mu_j$ converges weakly to $\nu$. Let $w_j\in L^2(\mu_j)$ and $f\in L^2(\nu)$. Suppose that $$\label{eq:polarization-norm} \int w_j^2\,d\mu_j\longrightarrow\int f^2\,d\nu$$ and, for every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\psi$, $$\label{eq:polarization-moment} \int w_j\psi\,d\mu_j\longrightarrow\int f\psi\,d\nu.$$ If $v:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R$ is globally Lipschitz and $f=v$ almost everywhere for $\nu$, then $$\int\|w_j-v\|^2\,d\mu_j\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Weak convergence of measures with convergent norms and tested moments gives strong L^2 convergence to a globally Lipschitz representative.
- **lem-normal-affine-planes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-affine-planes)): Let $\mu_j$ be measures, let the frames satisfy <a href="#eq:complete-normal-splitting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complete-normal-splitting">[eq:complete-normal-splitting]</a>, and suppose that $\delta_j>0$ tends to zero. Assume $$\sup_j\int\\|w_j\\|^2\,d\mu_j<\infty$$ and that, for one fixed affine map $v(x)=b+Bx$ from $\mathbb R^d$ to $\mathbb R^{q_0}$, each function $\\|w_j-v\\|^2$ is integrable and $$\int\\|w_j-v\\|^2\,d\mu_j\longrightarrow0.$$ Then there are affine $n$-planes $S'_j$ such that $$\label{eq:tilted-plane-distance-limit} \int\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,S'_j)}{\delta_j}\right)^2 d\mu_j(x)\longrightarrow0.$$ Every integral in this conclusion is finite. No convergence of the normal frames $N_j$ is required.
  - Explanation: Strong convergence of normal heights to an affine map yields explicit affine planes whose normalized mean-square distance tends to zero, without convergence of the normal frames.
- **prop-normalized-excess-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-normalized-excess-limit)): Fix $0<\gamma<1/2$. Let $\mu_j$ satisfy <a href="#eq:prop-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prop-upper">[eq:prop-upper]</a>–<a href="#eq:prop-riesz" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prop-riesz">[eq:prop-riesz]</a> with the same constants, and suppose that $0\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j$. Let $\delta_j>0$ and $K_j\in\mathbb N$ satisfy $$\label{eq:sequential-horizon-schedule} \delta_j\to0,\qquad K_j\to\infty,\qquad \delta_j2^{\gamma K_j}\to0,\qquad \delta_j^{-1}2^{-K_j}\to0.$$ Assume that $2^{K_j}\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_j)$ and $$\label{eq:sequential-horizon-excess} e_{\mu_j}(0,2^\ell)\le\delta_j2^{\gamma\ell} \qquad(0\le\ell\le K_j).$$ Put $A_j=8\,2^{K_j}$. Suppose, for every compactly supported 1-Lipschitz scalar function $\varphi$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\varphi\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\int\varphi\,d\mu_j=0$, that $$\label{eq:sequential-cubic-oscillation} \|\mathcal R_{\mu_j}(\varphi)\|\le\delta_j^3.$$ There is one subsequence along which, for every fixed $r>0$, $$\label{eq:sequential-excess-conclusion} \frac{e_{\mu_j}(0,r)}{\delta_j}\longrightarrow0, \qquad b_{\mu_j}(0,r)\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Along a subsequence, the excess divided by its initial scale and the bilateral flatness number both tend to zero on every fixed ball.
- **thm-excess-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-excess-propagation)): Fix $1\le n<d$, $c,G>0$, $D_0\ge0$, $0<\gamma<1/2$, and $\varepsilon>0$. For every integer $J_0\ge0$, there is an integer $J\ge J_0$, depending only on these fixed data, with the following property. Set $$\label{eq:uniform-propagation-constants} \delta=2^{-J},\qquad K=2J+3,\qquad A=8\,2^K.$$ Let $\mu$ satisfy <a href="#eq:prop-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prop-upper">[eq:prop-upper]</a>–<a href="#eq:prop-riesz" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prop-riesz">[eq:prop-riesz]</a>, let $a\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu$, and let $r>0$. Assume $$\begin{aligned} 2^Kr&\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu),\label{eq:prop-admissible-horizon}\\ e_\mu(a,2^\ell r)&\le\delta2^{\gamma\ell} &&(0\le\ell\le K),\label{eq:prop-horizon-assumption} \end{aligned}$$ and assume $$\label{eq:prop-oscillation-assumption} \|\mathcal R_\mu(\varphi)\|\le\delta^3r^n$$ for every compactly supported scalar function $\varphi$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\varphi)\le r^{-1},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\varphi\subset B(a,Ar),\qquad \int\varphi\,d\mu=0.$$ Then $$\label{eq:uniform-propagation-conclusion} e_\mu(a,r/2)\le\delta,\qquad b_\mu(a,r/2)<\varepsilon.$$ Equivalently, the oscillation hypothesis can be stated with $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\varphi)\le1$ and threshold $\delta^3r^{n+1}$. The constants $J,\delta,K,A$ are chosen before the measure, center, and radius. The test radius $Ar$ need not be admissible.
  - Explanation: Uniform improvement: small excess over a finite range of larger scales and small pairing oscillation give small excess and bilateral flatness at the next smaller scale, with constants fixed before the measure, center and radius.
- **lem-seed-charging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-charging)): Let $R\in\mathcal D$ and let $\mathcal F$ be a finite family of subcubes of $R$. For each $Q\in\mathcal F$, choose a cube $S(Q)\subset Q$ such that, for fixed $N\in\mathbb N$ and $M\geq1$, $$k(Q)\leq k(S(Q))\leq k(Q)+N, \qquad \mu(Q)\leq M\mu(S(Q)).$$ Let $\mathcal O\subset\mathcal D(R)$ satisfy $\sum_{T\in\mathcal O}\mu(T)\leq K\mu(R)$. Suppose that whenever $Q,Q'\in\mathcal F$ satisfy $$k(S(Q))<k(Q'),\qquad Q'\subset S(Q),$$ there is a cube $T\in\mathcal O$ with $$k(S(Q))\leq k(T)<k(Q'),\qquad Q'\subset T.$$ Then $$\sum_{Q\in\mathcal F}\mu(Q) \leq M(N+1)(1+K)\mu(R).$$
  - Explanation: A finite counting argument bounds the total mass of parents by the mass of the top cell times constants, charging gaps between selected seeds to a Carleson family.
- **prop-beta-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-beta-packing)): For each $H\geq4(C_0+1)$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a constant $C_{H,\varepsilon}<\infty$, depending only on $d,n,C_{\rm AD},C_{\rm R},H,\varepsilon$, such that $$\sum_{\substack{Q\subset R\\\beta_H(Q)\geq\varepsilon}}\mu(Q) \leq C_{H,\varepsilon}\mu(R) \qquad(R\in\mathcal D).$$
  - Explanation: Cells with large bilateral flatness number form a Carleson family, by chaining excess propagation from flat seeds and charging failures to oscillation-bad cells.
- **thm-bwgl-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bwgl)): Let $E\subset\mathbb R^d$ be closed, of positive diameter, and $n$-AD regular with respect to $\mathcal H^n$. Suppose that for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $C_\varepsilon<\infty$ such that $$\int_{E\cap B(a,R)}\int_0^R \mathbf1_{\{b_E(x,t)>\varepsilon\}}\,\frac{dt}{t}\,d\mathcal H^n(x) \leq C_\varepsilon R^n \quad(a\in E,\ 0<R<\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}E).$$ Then $E$ has big pieces of uniformly Lipschitz images: there are $\theta_0>0$ and $L<\infty$ such that for every $a\in E$ and $0<r<\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}E$ there is an $L$-Lipschitz map $f:B^n(0,r)\to\mathbb R^d$ for which $$\mathcal H^n\bigl(E\cap B(a,r)\cap f(B^n(0,r))\bigr)\geq\theta_0r^n.$$ The constants depend only on the dimensions, AD constants, and the constants in the bilateral weak geometric lemma.
  - Explanation: The David–Semmes bilateral criterion: Carleson-type control of bilateral flatness failures for an AD-regular closed set gives big pieces of uniformly Lipschitz images.
- **cor-variation-principal-values** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-variation)): Let $d\ge4$ and $2\le n\le d-2$ be integers, and let $\mu$ be an $n$-Ahlfors–David regular Radon measure satisfying <a href="#eq:riesz-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:riesz-bound">[eq:riesz-bound]</a>. Fix $\rho>2$ and a real odd $C^2$ convolution kernel $K$ satisfying <a href="#eq:variation-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:variation-kernel">[eq:variation-kernel]</a>. For every real $f\in L^2(\mu)$, $$\label{eq:hard-variation-consequence} \bigl\\|V_\rho((T^{K,\mu}_\varepsilon f)_{\varepsilon>0})\bigr\\|_{L^2(\mu)} \le C_2\\|f\\|_{L^2(\mu)}.$$ The principal value $$T^{K,\mu}f(x)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}T^{K,\mu}_\varepsilon f(x)$$ exists finitely for $\mu$-almost every $x$, belongs to $L^2(\mu)$, and $$\\|T^{K,\mu}_\varepsilon f-T^{K,\mu}f\\|_{L^2(\mu)} \longrightarrow0\qquad(\varepsilon\downarrow0).$$ The vector Riesz family $R_{\mu,\varepsilon}f$ satisfies the analogous hard-variation bound and has an almost-everywhere principal-value limit, with strong convergence in $L^2(\mu;\mathbb R^d)$. The exceptional null set may depend on $K$ and $f$. These are strong-convergence assertions on each input; no operator-norm convergence is asserted.* *For the fixed profile <a href="#eq:variation-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:variation-cutoff">[eq:variation-cutoff]</a>, every real $f\in L^p(\mu)$ with $1<p<\infty$ satisfies $$\label{eq:smooth-variation-consequence} \bigl\\|V_\rho((T^{K,\mu}_{\varphi,\varepsilon}f)_{\varepsilon>0}) \bigr\\|_{L^p(\mu)} \le C_p\\|f\\|_{L^p(\mu)}.$$ For every real $f\in L^1(\mu)$ and $\lambda>0$, $$\mu\left\{x: V_\rho((T^{K,\mu}_{\varphi,\varepsilon}f)_{\varepsilon>0})(x) >\lambda\right\} \le \frac{C_1}{\lambda}\\|f\\|_{L^1(\mu)}.$$ The constants may depend on the dimensions, the fixed $\rho$, the kernel bounds, the uniform-rectifiability data, and, for the smooth estimates, the chosen profile and $p$ when relevant.
  - Explanation: Under the main hypotheses, variation estimates, almost-everywhere principal values and strong convergence hold for odd kernels and the Riesz family, transferred from the Mas–Tolsa results.

        "exact_quote": "For every $A_{\\rm F}\\ge1$ and $\\eta>0$ there are an integer $N_{\\rm F}\\ge1$ and a Carleson family $\\mathcal F$ such that every $Q\\in\\mathcal D\\setminus\\mathcal F$ has a proper descendant $P\\subset Q$ with $$\\label{eq:flat-seed-conclusion}\n 1\\le\\operatorname{depth}_Q(P)\\le N_{\\rm F},\n \\qquad b_\\mu(z_P,A_{\\rm F}\\ell(P))<\\eta.$$ One may also require $B(z_P,A_{\\rm F}\\ell(P))\\subset B(z_Q,c_0\\ell(Q)/4)$. The constants are uniform in $Q$ and the top cube used in the Carleson estimate; they depend only on the fixed data, $A_{\\rm F}$ and $\\eta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-annular-alternative",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\kappa<1/8$, $\\varepsilon>0$, and $T>0$. There exists $\\rho\\in(0,\\kappa)$, depending only on $d,n,C_{\\rm AD},G,\\kappa,\\varepsilon,T$, with the following property. For every $a_0\\in E$ and $0<s\\leq D_E$, at least one of the following assertions holds:*\n\n1.  *There are $a\\in E\\cap B(a_0,\\kappa s)$ and $\\rho s\\leq t<\\kappa s$ such that $b_\\mu(a,t)<\\varepsilon$.*\n\n2.  *There are $a\\in E\\cap B(a_0,\\kappa s)$ and $\\rho s\\leq r\\leq R<\\kappa s$ such that $|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)|>T$.*\n\n*The number $\\rho$ is chosen before $\\mu$, $a_0$, and $s$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-annular-average-gap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<r\\le R$, let $S$ be a bounded measurable set containing $B(a,2R)$, and let $A\\subset B$ be measurable sets of positive finite measure satisfying $$A\\subset B(a,r/2),\\qquad B\\subset B(a,R/2),\\qquad\n r^n\\le M\\mu(A),\\qquad R^n\\le M\\mu(B).$$ There is a constant $$E_0=3C_{\\rm R}(G2^nM)^{1/2}+3C(n)G,$$ independent of $r,R,R/r,S$ and $\\varepsilon$, such that, for every $0<\\varepsilon<r/2$, $$\\label{eq:annular-average-error}\n \\left|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)\n -\\bigl(\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_A\n       -\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_B\\bigr)\\right|\n \\le E_0.$$ In particular, one direction chosen from $\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)$ witnesses an average gap at least $|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)|-E_0$ for every such $\\varepsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-annular-average-gap",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0<r\\le R$, let $S$ be a bounded measurable set containing $B(a,2R)$, and let $A\\subset B$ be measurable sets of positive finite measure satisfying $$A\\subset B(a,r/2),\\qquad B\\subset B(a,R/2),\\qquad\n r^n\\le M\\mu(A),\\qquad R^n\\le M\\mu(B).$$ There is a constant $$E_0=3C_{\\rm R}(G2^nM)^{1/2}+3C(n)G,$$ independent of $r,R,R/r,S$ and $\\varepsilon$, such that, for every $0<\\varepsilon<r/2$, $$\\label{eq:annular-average-error}\n \\left|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)\n -\\bigl(\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_A\n       -\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_B\\bigr)\\right|\n \\le E_0.$$ In particular, one direction chosen from $\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)$ witnesses an average gap at least $|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)|-E_0$ for every such $\\varepsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-081.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $0<r\\le R$, let $S$ be a bounded measurable set containing $B(a,2R)$, and let $A\\subset B$ be measurable sets of positive finite measure satisfying $$A\\subset B(a,r/2),\\qquad B\\subset B(a,R/2),\\qquad\n r^n\\le M\\mu(A),\\qquad R^n\\le M\\mu(B).$$ There is a constant $$E_0=3C_{\\rm R}(G2^nM)^{1/2}+3C(n)G,$$ independent of $r,R,R/r,S$ and $\\varepsilon$, such that, for every $0<\\varepsilon<r/2$, $$\\label{eq:annular-average-error}\n \\left|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)\n -\\bigl(\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_A\n       -\\langle R_{\\mu,\\varepsilon}1_S\\rangle_B\\bigr)\\right|\n \\le E_0.$$ In particular, one direction chosen from $\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)$ witnesses an average gap at least $|\\mathcal S_\\mu(a;r,R)|-E_0$ for every such $\\varepsilon$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-plane-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le n\\le d$, $G\\ge0$, and $c>0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $0<\\varepsilon_0\\le1$, depending only on $n,d,G,c$, with the following property. Suppose that $\\sigma$ has global upper $n$-growth $G$, $R>0$, and $\\sigma(B(0,R))\\ge cR^n$. If $S,W$ are affine $n$-planes and $0<\\eta\\le\\varepsilon_0R$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:joint-plane-error}\n \\int_{B(0,R)}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)^2\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma(x)\n \\le \\frac c2\\eta^2R^n,$$ then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)&\\le A\\eta(1+|x|/R) &&(x\\in S),\\label{eq:plane-comparison}\\\\\n |\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)|&\\le A\\eta(3+|x|/R)\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d).\\label{eq:distance-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first conclusion also holds with $S$ and $W$ interchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-plane-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le n\\le d$, $G\\ge0$, and $c>0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $0<\\varepsilon_0\\le1$, depending only on $n,d,G,c$, with the following property. Suppose that $\\sigma$ has global upper $n$-growth $G$, $R>0$, and $\\sigma(B(0,R))\\ge cR^n$. If $S,W$ are affine $n$-planes and $0<\\eta\\le\\varepsilon_0R$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:joint-plane-error}\n \\int_{B(0,R)}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)^2\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma(x)\n \\le \\frac c2\\eta^2R^n,$$ then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)&\\le A\\eta(1+|x|/R) &&(x\\in S),\\label{eq:plane-comparison}\\\\\n |\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)|&\\le A\\eta(3+|x|/R)\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d).\\label{eq:distance-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first conclusion also holds with $S$ and $W$ interchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-081.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $1\\le n\\le d$, $G\\ge0$, and $c>0$. There are constants $A>0$ and $0<\\varepsilon_0\\le1$, depending only on $n,d,G,c$, with the following property. Suppose that $\\sigma$ has global upper $n$-growth $G$, $R>0$, and $\\sigma(B(0,R))\\ge cR^n$. If $S,W$ are affine $n$-planes and $0<\\eta\\le\\varepsilon_0R$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:joint-plane-error}\n \\int_{B(0,R)}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)^2\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma(x)\n \\le \\frac c2\\eta^2R^n,$$ then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)&\\le A\\eta(1+|x|/R) &&(x\\in S),\\label{eq:plane-comparison}\\\\\n |\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,W)|&\\le A\\eta(3+|x|/R)\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d).\\label{eq:distance-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first conclusion also holds with $S$ and $W$ interchanged.",
      "explanation": "Two affine planes that both fit the measure in the mean-square sense on a ball with small error stay close, with a linear growth bound.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-plane-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\gamma<1/2$ and the constants $n,d,G,c$ of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:plane-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:plane-comparison\">14</a>. There are $\\kappa>0$ and $M<\\infty$, depending only on these data and $\\gamma$, as follows. Suppose $K\\ge0$ is an integer, $\\delta>0$, $\\delta2^{\\gamma K}\\le\\kappa$, $\\sigma$ has global upper growth $G$, and, for $0\\le k\\le K$, $$\\sigma(B(0,2^k))\\ge c2^{kn},\n \\qquad e_\\sigma(0,2^k)\\le\\delta2^{\\gamma k}.$$ Then an affine $n$-plane $S$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:fixed-plane-moments}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2\\,d\\sigma(x)\n \\le M\\delta^2 2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-plane-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\gamma<1/2$ and the constants $n,d,G,c$ of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:plane-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:plane-comparison\">14</a>. Suppose $K\\ge0$ is an integer, $\\delta>0$, $\\delta2^{\\gamma K}\\le\\kappa$, $\\sigma$ has global upper growth $G$, and, for $0\\le k\\le K$, $$\\sigma(B(0,2^k))\\ge c2^{kn},\n \\qquad e_\\sigma(0,2^k)\\le\\delta2^{\\gamma k}.$$ Then an affine $n$-plane $S$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:fixed-plane-moments}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S)^2\\,d\\sigma(x)\n \\le M\\delta^2 2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-planar-rigidity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le n\\le d$, let $L$ be an affine $n$-plane, and let $\\sigma$ be a nonzero Radon measure on $\\mathbb R^d$ supported in $L$. Suppose that $c,G>0$ and $$\\sigma(B(x,r))\\le Gr^n\\quad(x\\in\\mathbb R^d,\\ r>0),\n \\qquad\n \\sigma(B(x,r))\\ge cr^n\\quad(x\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma,\\ r>0).$$ Suppose also that $\\mathcal R_\\sigma(\\varphi)=0$ for every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\varphi$ with $\\int\\varphi\\,d\\sigma=0$. Assume the hard Riesz truncations are uniformly bounded on $L^2(\\sigma)$. Then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma=L,\n \\qquad \\sigma=\\theta\\,\\mathcal H^n\\!\\restriction L\n \\quad\\text{for some }\\theta>0.$$ The same conclusion holds if the operator assumption on $\\sigma$ is replaced by the following source assumption: $\\sigma_j$ are Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ converging to $\\sigma$ against compact continuous tests, with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common uniform hard-truncation $L^2$ bound. With the Euclidean normalization of $\\mathcal H^n$, the resulting constant satisfies $c/\\omega_n\\le\\theta\\le G/\\omega_n$, where $\\omega_n=|B_{\\mathbb R^n}(0,1)|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-planar-rigidity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le n\\le d$, let $L$ be an affine $n$-plane, and let $\\sigma$ be a nonzero Radon measure on $\\mathbb R^d$ supported in $L$. Suppose that $c,G>0$ and $$\\sigma(B(x,r))\\le Gr^n\\quad(x\\in\\mathbb R^d,\\ r>0),\n \\qquad\n \\sigma(B(x,r))\\ge cr^n\\quad(x\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma,\\ r>0).$$ Suppose also that $\\mathcal R_\\sigma(\\varphi)=0$ for every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\varphi$ with $\\int\\varphi\\,d\\sigma=0$. Assume the hard Riesz truncations are uniformly bounded on $L^2(\\sigma)$. Then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma=L,\n \\qquad \\sigma=\\theta\\,\\mathcal H^n\\!\\restriction L\n \\quad\\text{for some }\\theta>0.$$ The same conclusion holds if the operator assumption on $\\sigma$ is replaced by the following source assumption: $\\sigma_j$ are Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ converging to $\\sigma$ against compact continuous tests, with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common uniform hard-truncation $L^2$ bound. With the Euclidean normalization of $\\mathcal H^n$, the resulting constant satisfies $c/\\omega_n\\le\\theta\\le G/\\omega_n$, where $\\omega_n=|B_{\\mathbb R^n}(0,1)|$.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $1\\le n\\le d$, let $L$ be an affine $n$-plane, and let $\\sigma$ be a nonzero Radon measure on $\\mathbb R^d$ supported in $L$. Assume the hard Riesz truncations are uniformly bounded on $L^2(\\sigma)$. Then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma=L,\n \\qquad \\sigma=\\theta\\,\\mathcal H^n\\!\\restriction L\n \\quad\\text{for some }\\theta>0.$$ The same conclusion holds if the operator assumption on $\\sigma$ is replaced by the following source assumption: $\\sigma_j$ are Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ converging to $\\sigma$ against compact continuous tests, with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common uniform hard-truncation $L^2$ bound. With the Euclidean normalization of $\\mathcal H^n$, the resulting constant satisfies $c/\\omega_n\\le\\theta\\le G/\\omega_n$, where $\\omega_n=|B_{\\mathbb R^n}(0,1)|$.",
      "explanation": "A reflectionless measure supported in an affine plane with two-sided growth and a uniform operator bound has full support and constant density on that plane.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-flat-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le n<d$, $c>0$, $0<G<\\infty$, $0\\le D_0<\\infty$, and $0<\\gamma<1/2$. Let $\\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ with $0\\in E_j=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$, global upper $n$-growth $G$, and $$\\mu_j(B(x,r))\\ge cr^n\n \\quad(x\\in E_j,\\ 0<r\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}E_j).$$ Assume every hard-truncated Riesz transform on $L^2(\\mu_j)$ has norm at most $D_0$. Let $\\delta_j>0$, integers $K_j\\ge0$, and $A_j,v_j>0$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-horizons}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\delta_j\\longrightarrow0,\\quad K_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad\n \\delta_j2^{\\gamma K_j}\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n 2^{K_j}\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}E_j,\\quad A_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad v_j\\longrightarrow0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Suppose $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-excess}\n e_{\\mu_j}(0,2^k)\\le\\delta_j2^{\\gamma k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j)$$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-oscillation}\n |\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\varphi)|\\le v_j\n \\quad\\text{if }\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}\\varphi\\le1,\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\varphi\\Subset B(0,A_j),\\quad\\int\\varphi\\,d\\mu_j=0.$$ There is a subsequence, relabeled by $j$, with the following properties. All the input sequences are restricted along this same subsequence when we relabel them. For each index there are an affine plane $S_j=a_j+L_j(\\mathbb R^n)$, tangent and normal linear isometries $$L_j:\\mathbb R^n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad N_j:\\mathbb R^{d-n}\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad L_jL_j^*+N_jN_j^*=I,$$ such that $a_j$ is the point of $S_j$ nearest $0$, $a_j\\to0$, and $L_j\\to L_\\infty$ in operator norm, where $L_\\infty$ is an isometry. Along this same subsequence, $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-measure}\n \\mu_j\\rightharpoonup q(L_\\infty)_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n \\qquad \\frac{c}{4^n\\omega_n}\\le q\\le\\frac{2^nG}{\\omega_n},\n \\qquad \\omega_n=\\mathcal L^n(B(0,1)).$$ Here convergence is against continuous compactly supported tests, and $\\mathcal L^n$ in <a href=\"#eq:flat-limit-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flat-limit-measure\">[eq:flat-limit-measure]</a> denotes Lebesgue measure. The planes obey <a href=\"#eq:fixed-plane-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fixed-plane-moments\">[eq:fixed-plane-moments]</a> with a single constant $M=M(n,d,c,G,\\gamma)$. In particular, writing $e_i$ for the standard basis of $\\mathbb R^{d-n}$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-coordinate}\n u_{j,i}(x)=\\langle N_je_i,x-a_j\\rangle,$$ we have $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-moments}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_{j,i}|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j, 1\\le i\\le d-n).$$ Moreover, on every bounded ball, the supports converge bilaterally to $L_\\infty(\\mathbb R^n)$: each source support point approaches that plane uniformly, and each point of the plane approaches $E_j$ uniformly.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },

          "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le n<d$, $c>0$, $0<G<\\infty$, $0\\le D_0<\\infty$, and $0<\\gamma<1/2$. Let $\\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ with $0\\in E_j=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$, global upper $n$-growth $G$, and $$\\mu_j(B(x,r))\\ge cr^n\n \\quad(x\\in E_j,\\ 0<r\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}E_j).$$ Assume every hard-truncated Riesz transform on $L^2(\\mu_j)$ has norm at most $D_0$. Let $\\delta_j>0$, integers $K_j\\ge0$, and $A_j,v_j>0$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-horizons}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\delta_j\\longrightarrow0,\\quad K_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad\n \\delta_j2^{\\gamma K_j}\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n 2^{K_j}\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}E_j,\\quad A_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad v_j\\longrightarrow0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Suppose $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-excess}\n e_{\\mu_j}(0,2^k)\\le\\delta_j2^{\\gamma k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j)$$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-oscillation}\n |\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\varphi)|\\le v_j\n \\quad\\text{if }\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}\\varphi\\le1,\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\varphi\\Subset B(0,A_j),\\quad\\int\\varphi\\,d\\mu_j=0.$$ There is a subsequence, relabeled by $j$, with the following properties. All the input sequences are restricted along this same subsequence when we relabel them. For each index there are an affine plane $S_j=a_j+L_j(\\mathbb R^n)$, tangent and normal linear isometries $$L_j:\\mathbb R^n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad N_j:\\mathbb R^{d-n}\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad L_jL_j^*+N_jN_j^*=I,$$ such that $a_j$ is the point of $S_j$ nearest $0$, $a_j\\to0$, and $L_j\\to L_\\infty$ in operator norm, where $L_\\infty$ is an isometry. Along this same subsequence, $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-measure}\n \\mu_j\\rightharpoonup q(L_\\infty)_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n \\qquad \\frac{c}{4^n\\omega_n}\\le q\\le\\frac{2^nG}{\\omega_n},\n \\qquad \\omega_n=\\mathcal L^n(B(0,1)).$$ Here convergence is against continuous compactly supported tests, and $\\mathcal L^n$ in <a href=\"#eq:flat-limit-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flat-limit-measure\">[eq:flat-limit-measure]</a> denotes Lebesgue measure. The planes obey <a href=\"#eq:fixed-plane-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fixed-plane-moments\">[eq:fixed-plane-moments]</a> with a single constant $M=M(n,d,c,G,\\gamma)$. In particular, writing $e_i$ for the standard basis of $\\mathbb R^{d-n}$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-coordinate}\n u_{j,i}(x)=\\langle N_je_i,x-a_j\\rangle,$$ we have $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-moments}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_{j,i}|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j, 1\\le i\\le d-n).$$ Moreover, on every bounded ball, the supports converge bilaterally to $L_\\infty(\\mathbb R^n)$: each source support point approaches that plane uniformly, and each point of the plane approaches $E_j$ uniformly.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $1\\le n<d$, $c>0$, $0<G<\\infty$, $0\\le D_0<\\infty$, and $0<\\gamma<1/2$. Let $\\delta_j>0$, integers $K_j\\ge0$, and $A_j,v_j>0$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-horizons}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\delta_j\\longrightarrow0,\\quad K_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad\n \\delta_j2^{\\gamma K_j}\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n 2^{K_j}\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}E_j,\\quad A_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad v_j\\longrightarrow0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Suppose $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-excess}\n e_{\\mu_j}(0,2^k)\\le\\delta_j2^{\\gamma k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j)$$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-source-oscillation}\n |\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\varphi)|\\le v_j\n \\quad\\text{if }\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}\\varphi\\le1,\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\varphi\\Subset B(0,A_j),\\quad\\int\\varphi\\,d\\mu_j=0.$$ There is a subsequence, relabeled by $j$, with the following properties. All the input sequences are restricted along this same subsequence when we relabel them. For each index there are an affine plane $S_j=a_j+L_j(\\mathbb R^n)$, tangent and normal linear isometries $$L_j:\\mathbb R^n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad N_j:\\mathbb R^{d-n}\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d,\n \\qquad L_jL_j^*+N_jN_j^*=I,$$ such that $a_j$ is the point of $S_j$ nearest $0$, $a_j\\to0$, and $L_j\\to L_\\infty$ in operator norm, where $L_\\infty$ is an isometry. Along this same subsequence, $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-measure}\n \\mu_j\\rightharpoonup q(L_\\infty)_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n \\qquad \\frac{c}{4^n\\omega_n}\\le q\\le\\frac{2^nG}{\\omega_n},\n \\qquad \\omega_n=\\mathcal L^n(B(0,1)).$$ Here convergence is against continuous compactly supported tests, and $\\mathcal L^n$ in <a href=\"#eq:flat-limit-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flat-limit-measure\">[eq:flat-limit-measure]</a> denotes Lebesgue measure. The planes obey <a href=\"#eq:fixed-plane-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fixed-plane-moments\">[eq:fixed-plane-moments]</a> with a single constant $M=M(n,d,c,G,\\gamma)$. In particular, writing $e_i$ for the standard basis of $\\mathbb R^{d-n}$ and $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-coordinate}\n u_{j,i}(x)=\\langle N_je_i,x-a_j\\rangle,$$ we have $$\\label{eq:flat-limit-normal-moments}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_{j,i}|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j, 1\\le i\\le d-n).$$ Moreover, on every bounded ball, the supports converge bilaterally to $L_\\infty(\\mathbb R^n)$: each source support point approaches that plane uniformly, and each point of the plane approaches $E_j$ uniformly.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le n<d$, $0<\\gamma<1$, and let $\\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ with $0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$. Suppose that, for fixed $G,c>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_j(B(x,r))&\\le Gr^n\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d, r>0),\\notag\\\\\n \\mu_j(B(0,r))&\\ge cr^n\n       &&(0<r\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j),\n \\label{eq:height-growth}\n\\end{aligned}$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$. Let $u_j$ be real affine functions with $$u_j(x)-u_j(y)=\\langle e_j,x-y\\rangle,\n \\qquad |e_j|\\le1,\\qquad |u_j(0)|\\le W.\n \\label{eq:height-affine-data}$$ Assume $\\delta_j>0$, $\\delta_j\\to0$, $K_j\\to\\infty$, and $$\\frac{2^{-K_j}}{\\delta_j}\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:height-last-radius}$$ Suppose, with a fixed $M<\\infty$, that $$\\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{(n+2+2\\gamma)k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j).\n \\label{eq:height-dyadic-moments}$$ Finally, let $A_j\\to\\infty$ and suppose that every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\phi$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\phi\\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\\int\\phi\\,d\\mu_j=0$ satisfies $$|\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\phi)|\n       \\le \\delta_j^3\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:height-small-oscillation}$$ Then $w_j=u_j/\\delta_j$ has finite local energy, and for every $H<\\infty$ there is $C_H<\\infty$, independent of $j$, such that $$\\int_{B(0,H)}|w_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,H)}(w_j)\\le C_H.\n \\label{eq:height-uniform-energy}$$ The eventual bound depends only on $H,n,\\gamma,G,c,M,W$; finitely many initial terms may be included by enlarging $C_H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le n<d$, $0<\\gamma<1$, and let $\\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ with $0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$. Suppose that, for fixed $G,c>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_j(B(x,r))&\\le Gr^n\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d, r>0),\\notag\\\\\n \\mu_j(B(0,r))&\\ge cr^n\n       &&(0<r\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j),\n \\label{eq:height-growth}\n\\end{aligned}$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$. Let $u_j$ be real affine functions with $$u_j(x)-u_j(y)=\\langle e_j,x-y\\rangle,\n \\qquad |e_j|\\le1,\\qquad |u_j(0)|\\le W.\n \\label{eq:height-affine-data}$$ Assume $\\delta_j>0$, $\\delta_j\\to0$, $K_j\\to\\infty$, and $$\\frac{2^{-K_j}}{\\delta_j}\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:height-last-radius}$$ Suppose, with a fixed $M<\\infty$, that $$\\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{(n+2+2\\gamma)k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j).\n \\label{eq:height-dyadic-moments}$$ Finally, let $A_j\\to\\infty$ and suppose that every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\phi$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\phi\\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\\int\\phi\\,d\\mu_j=0$ satisfies $$|\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\phi)|\n       \\le \\delta_j^3\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:height-small-oscillation}$$ Then $w_j=u_j/\\delta_j$ has finite local energy, and for every $H<\\infty$ there is $C_H<\\infty$, independent of $j$, such that $$\\int_{B(0,H)}|w_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,H)}(w_j)\\le C_H.\n \\label{eq:height-uniform-energy}$$ The eventual bound depends only on $H,n,\\gamma,G,c,M,W$; finitely many initial terms may be included by enlarging $C_H$.",
          "source_id": "PM-081.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $1\\le n<d$, $0<\\gamma<1$, and let $\\mu_j$ be Radon measures on $\\mathbb R^d$ with $0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$. Suppose that, for fixed $G,c>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_j(B(x,r))&\\le Gr^n\n       &&(x\\in\\mathbb R^d, r>0),\\notag\\\\\n \\mu_j(B(0,r))&\\ge cr^n\n       &&(0<r\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j),\n \\label{eq:height-growth}\n\\end{aligned}$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$. Let $u_j$ be real affine functions with $$u_j(x)-u_j(y)=\\langle e_j,x-y\\rangle,\n \\qquad |e_j|\\le1,\\qquad |u_j(0)|\\le W.\n \\label{eq:height-affine-data}$$ Assume $\\delta_j>0$, $\\delta_j\\to0$, $K_j\\to\\infty$, and $$\\frac{2^{-K_j}}{\\delta_j}\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:height-last-radius}$$ Suppose, with a fixed $M<\\infty$, that $$\\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n \\le M\\delta_j^2 2^{(n+2+2\\gamma)k}\n \\qquad(0\\le k\\le K_j).\n \\label{eq:height-dyadic-moments}$$ Finally, let $A_j\\to\\infty$ and suppose that every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\phi$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\phi\\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\\int\\phi\\,d\\mu_j=0$ satisfies $$|\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\phi)|\n       \\le \\delta_j^3\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:height-small-oscillation}$$ Then $w_j=u_j/\\delta_j$ has finite local energy, and for every $H<\\infty$ there is $C_H<\\infty$, independent of $j$, such that $$\\int_{B(0,H)}|w_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,H)}(w_j)\\le C_H.\n \\label{eq:height-uniform-energy}$$ The eventual bound depends only on $H,n,\\gamma,G,c,M,W$; finitely many initial terms may be included by enlarging $C_H$.",
      "explanation": "Normalized affine heights have uniformly bounded local second moment and fractional energy, obtained by testing the small pairing against a centered cutoff and bounding the exterior remainder.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-energy",

      "id": "lem-height-energy",

      "quantifiers": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-energy",
          "exact_quote": "for every $H<\\infty$ there is $C_H<\\infty$, independent of $j$,",

      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-height-energy"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-moving-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\mu_j$ are Radon measures with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common lower bound $cr^n$ at every support point and every admissible radius. Suppose also that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$ and $$\\mu_j\\longrightarrow\\nu=q\\,e_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n       \\qquad q>0,\n \\label{eq:height-flat-measure-limit}$$ against continuous compactly supported tests. Let $w_j^1,\\ldots,w_j^s$ be finitely many real measurable functions. Assume that each belongs to $L^2$ on bounded balls and has integrable local fractional energy, and that for every $R<\\infty$ $$\\sup_j\\sum_{a=1}^s\n \\left(\\int_{B(0,R)}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n        +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,R)}(w_j^a)\\right)<\\infty.\n \\label{eq:height-compactness-hypothesis}$$ There are one subsequence and measurable functions $v^1,\\ldots,v^s\\in L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(L,\\nu)$ such that, on that subsequence, for every $H$ and every continuous function $\\psi$ on the compact set $\\overline\\Omega_H$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}w_j^a\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}v^a\\psi\\,d\\nu,\n \\label{eq:height-signed-moment-limit}\\\\\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-square-moment-limit}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The ordinary restrictions $\\mu_j|_{\\Omega_H}$ also converge weakly to $\\nu|_{\\Omega_H}$ as finite measures on $\\overline\\Omega_H$. In particular, $$\\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       \\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-norm-limit}$$ The subsequence and the functions are common to all these localizations and tests.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-moving-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\mu_j$ are Radon measures with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common lower bound $cr^n$ at every support point and every admissible radius. Suppose also that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$ and $$\\mu_j\\longrightarrow\\nu=q\\,e_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n       \\qquad q>0,\n \\label{eq:height-flat-measure-limit}$$ against continuous compactly supported tests. Let $w_j^1,\\ldots,w_j^s$ be finitely many real measurable functions. Assume that each belongs to $L^2$ on bounded balls and has integrable local fractional energy, and that for every $R<\\infty$ $$\\sup_j\\sum_{a=1}^s\n \\left(\\int_{B(0,R)}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n        +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,R)}(w_j^a)\\right)<\\infty.\n \\label{eq:height-compactness-hypothesis}$$ There are one subsequence and measurable functions $v^1,\\ldots,v^s\\in L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(L,\\nu)$ such that, on that subsequence, for every $H$ and every continuous function $\\psi$ on the compact set $\\overline\\Omega_H$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}w_j^a\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}v^a\\psi\\,d\\nu,\n \\label{eq:height-signed-moment-limit}\\\\\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-square-moment-limit}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The ordinary restrictions $\\mu_j|_{\\Omega_H}$ also converge weakly to $\\nu|_{\\Omega_H}$ as finite measures on $\\overline\\Omega_H$. In particular, $$\\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       \\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-norm-limit}$$ The subsequence and the functions are common to all these localizations and tests.",
          "source_id": "PM-081.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $\\mu_j$ are Radon measures with a common global upper $n$-growth bound and a common lower bound $cr^n$ at every support point and every admissible radius. Suppose also that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j\\to\\infty$ and $$\\mu_j\\longrightarrow\\nu=q\\,e_\\#\\mathcal L^n,\n       \\qquad q>0,\n \\label{eq:height-flat-measure-limit}$$ against continuous compactly supported tests. Let $w_j^1,\\ldots,w_j^s$ be finitely many real measurable functions. Assume that each belongs to $L^2$ on bounded balls and has integrable local fractional energy, and that for every $R<\\infty$ $$\\sup_j\\sum_{a=1}^s\n \\left(\\int_{B(0,R)}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n        +\\mathcal E_{\\mu_j,B(0,R)}(w_j^a)\\right)<\\infty.\n \\label{eq:height-compactness-hypothesis}$$ There are one subsequence and measurable functions $v^1,\\ldots,v^s\\in L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(L,\\nu)$ such that, on that subsequence, for every $H$ and every continuous function $\\psi$ on the compact set $\\overline\\Omega_H$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}w_j^a\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}v^a\\psi\\,d\\nu,\n \\label{eq:height-signed-moment-limit}\\\\\n \\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\n       &\\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\psi\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-square-moment-limit}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The ordinary restrictions $\\mu_j|_{\\Omega_H}$ also converge weakly to $\\nu|_{\\Omega_H}$ as finite measures on $\\overline\\Omega_H$. In particular, $$\\int_{\\Omega_H}|w_j^a|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       \\longrightarrow\\int_{\\Omega_H}|v^a|^2\\,d\\nu.\n \\label{eq:height-norm-limit}$$ The subsequence and the functions are common to all these localizations and tests.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-height-equation",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses and conclusions of Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:height-energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:height-energy\">18</a> and <a href=\"#lem:moving-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:moving-compactness\">19</a> for a scalar height. We retain their notation and record the conditions used below. Let $\\mu_j$ converge on compact tests to $\\nu=q\\mathcal H^n|_L$, where $q>0$ and $L$ is an $n$-plane through $0$. Here $\\mathcal H^n|_L$ has Euclidean normalization, equal to Lebesgue measure in orthonormal coordinates on $L$. Let $u_j$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:affine-source-height\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:affine-source-height\">[eq:affine-source-height]</a> with $|e_j|\\le1$, let $w_j=u_j/\\delta_j$, and let $v$ be the common limit given by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:moving-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:moving-compactness\">19</a>. Suppose $\\delta_j>0$, $\\delta_j\\to0$, $K_j\\to\\infty$, and, for some fixed $0<\\gamma<1$ and $M<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:height-source-moment}\n \\int_{B(0,2^k)}|u_j|^2\\,d\\mu_j\n       \\le M\\delta_j^2\\,2^{k(n+2+2\\gamma)}\n       \\quad(0\\le k\\le K_j),\\qquad\n \\delta_j^{-1}2^{-K_j}\\longrightarrow0.$$ Assume that there are radii $A_j\\to\\infty$ such that, for every $j$ and every compact Lipschitz test $\\xi$ supported in $B(0,A_j)$ with $\\int\\xi\\,d\\mu_j=0$, $$\\label{eq:height-source-oscillation}\n |\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\xi)|\n      \\le C(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}\\xi+1)\\delta_j^3.$$ The constant here is independent of $j$ and of the test.*\n\n\n\n*Then $v$ has finite local fractional energy and satisfies $$\\label{eq:limit-height-weight}\n \\int_L |v(x)|(1+|x|)^{-n-2}\\,d\\mathcal H^n(x)<\\infty.$$ After identifying $L$ isometrically with $\\mathbb R^n$, for every compactly supported smooth complex function $g$ with $\\int_{\\mathbb R^n}g=0$, $$\\label{eq:limit-fractional-equation}\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^n}v(x)\\mathcal Lg(x)\\,dx=0,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal Lg(x)=\\frac12\\int_{\\mathbb R^n}\n       \\frac{2g(x)-g(x+h)-g(x-h)}{|h|^{n+1}}\\,dh.$$ The integral on the left is absolutely convergent. The same conclusions hold simultaneously for the finite family of normal heights in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:moving-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:moving-compactness\">19</a>, without further extraction.",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fractional-rigidity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$, and let $v:\\mathbb R^n\\to\\mathbb R$ be measurable with $$\\label{eq:rigidity-weighted-tail}\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^n}|v(x)|(1+|x|)^{-n-2}\\,dx<\\infty.$$ Suppose that, for every $g\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^n;\\mathbb C)$ with $\\int g=0$, $$\\label{eq:rigidity-compact-equation}\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^n}v(x)\\mathcal Lg(x)\\,dx=0.$$ Then there are $b\\in\\mathbb R$ and a real linear functional $\\ell:\\mathbb R^n\\to\\mathbb R$ such that $v(x)=b+\\ell(x)$ almost everywhere. If $v$ is also essentially bounded, then $\\ell=0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-affine-polarization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_j$ and $\\nu$ be finite measures on $\\mathbb R^d$, all concentrated on one bounded set, and suppose that $\\mu_j$ converges weakly to $\\nu$. Let $w_j\\in L^2(\\mu_j)$ and $f\\in L^2(\\nu)$. Suppose that $$\\label{eq:polarization-norm}\n \\int w_j^2\\,d\\mu_j\\longrightarrow\\int f^2\\,d\\nu$$ and, for every compactly supported Lipschitz function $\\psi$, $$\\label{eq:polarization-moment}\n \\int w_j\\psi\\,d\\mu_j\\longrightarrow\\int f\\psi\\,d\\nu.$$ If $v:\\mathbb R^d\\to\\mathbb R$ is globally Lipschitz and $f=v$ almost everywhere for $\\nu$, then $$\\int|w_j-v|^2\\,d\\mu_j\\longrightarrow0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-081.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-affine-polarization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_j$ and $\\nu$ be finite measures on $\\mathbb R^d$, all concentrated on one bounded set, and suppose that $\\mu_j$ converges weakly to $\\nu$.           "source_id": "PM-081.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mu_j$ and $\\nu$ be finite measures on $\\mathbb R^d$, all concentrated on one bounded set, and suppose that $\\mu_j$ converges weakly to $\\nu$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-affine-planes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_j$ be measures, let the frames satisfy <a href=\"#eq:complete-normal-splitting\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:complete-normal-splitting\">[eq:complete-normal-splitting]</a>, and suppose that $\\delta_j>0$ tends to zero. Assume $$\\sup_j\\int\\|w_j\\|^2\\,d\\mu_j<\\infty$$ and that, for one fixed affine map $v(x)=b+Bx$ from $\\mathbb R^d$ to $\\mathbb R^{q_0}$, each function $\\|w_j-v\\|^2$ is integrable and $$\\int\\|w_j-v\\|^2\\,d\\mu_j\\longrightarrow0.$$ Then there are affine $n$-planes $S'_j$ such that $$\\label{eq:tilted-plane-distance-limit}\n \\int\\left(\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,S'_j)}{\\delta_j}\\right)^2\n       d\\mu_j(x)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Every integral in this conclusion is finite.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalized-excess-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\gamma<1/2$. Let $\\mu_j$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:prop-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prop-upper\">[eq:prop-upper]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:prop-riesz\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prop-riesz\">[eq:prop-riesz]</a> with the same constants, and suppose that $0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j$. Let $\\delta_j>0$ and $K_j\\in\\mathbb N$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:sequential-horizon-schedule}\n \\delta_j\\to0,\\qquad K_j\\to\\infty,\\qquad\n \\delta_j2^{\\gamma K_j}\\to0,\\qquad\n \\delta_j^{-1}2^{-K_j}\\to0.$$ Assume that $2^{K_j}\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}(\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu_j)$ and $$\\label{eq:sequential-horizon-excess}\n e_{\\mu_j}(0,2^\\ell)\\le\\delta_j2^{\\gamma\\ell}\n       \\qquad(0\\le\\ell\\le K_j).$$ Put $A_j=8\\,2^{K_j}$. Suppose, for every compactly supported 1-Lipschitz scalar function $\\varphi$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\varphi\\subset B(0,A_j)$ and $\\int\\varphi\\,d\\mu_j=0$, that $$\\label{eq:sequential-cubic-oscillation}\n |\\mathcal R_{\\mu_j}(\\varphi)|\\le\\delta_j^3.$$ There is one subsequence along which, for every fixed $r>0$, $$\\label{eq:sequential-excess-conclusion}\n \\frac{e_{\\mu_j}(0,r)}{\\delta_j}\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad b_{\\mu_j}(0,r)\\longrightarrow0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-excess-propagation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le n<d$, $c,G>0$, $D_0\\ge0$, $0<\\gamma<1/2$, and $\\varepsilon>0$.         "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be closed, of positive diameter, and $n$-AD regular with respect to $\\mathcal H^n$.         "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge4$ and $2\\le n\\le d-2$ be integers, and let $\\mu$ be an $n$-Ahlfors–David regular Radon measure satisfying <a href=\"#eq:riesz-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:riesz-bound\">[eq:riesz-bound]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **n-Ahlfors–David regular measure** ([source](#stmt-def-ad)): A Radon measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^d$ is *$n$-Ahlfors–David regular* with constant $C_{\rm AD}\ge1$ if $$C_{\rm AD}^{-1}r^n\le\mu(B(x,r))\le C_{\rm AD}r^n \qquad(x\in E,\quad 0<r\le D_E).$$ We call these positive radii *admissible*. The inequalities have no instances when $D_E=0$.
- **uniformly n-rectifiable measure** ([source](#stmt-def-ur)): An $n$-Ahlfors–David regular measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^d$ is *uniformly $n$-rectifiable* if there are $\theta>0$ and $M<\infty$ such that, for every $x\in E$ and $0<r\le D_E$, there is an $M$-Lipschitz map $$g:B_{\mathbb R^n}(0,r)\longrightarrow\mathbb R^d, \qquad \mu\bigl(B(x,r)\cap g(B_{\mathbb R^n}(0,r))\bigr)\ge\theta r^n.$$ The constants are chosen before $x$ and $r$.
- **excess and bilateral flatness number** ([source](#stmt-def-flatness)): For $r>0$ and a measure $\sigma$ with support $F$, let $$\begin{aligned} e_\sigma(a,r) &=\inf_L\left(r^{-n-2}\int_{B(a,r)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,L)^2\,d\sigma(x)\right)^{1/2},\\ b_\sigma(a,r) &=\inf_L\frac1r\left( \sup_{x\in F\cap B(a,r)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,L) +\sup_{y\in L\cap B(a,r)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(y,F)\right). \end{aligned}$$ Both infima run over affine $n$-planes in $\mathbb R^d$, and an empty supremum is zero. We call $e$ the *excess* and $b$ the *bilateral flatness number*.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-global-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-growth): Covering of the support by separated balls and volume comparison, then dyadic annuli for the kernel. -> Global upper growth and absolute convergence of truncations
- **step-pairings** [claim](#stmt-lem-pairings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pairings): Dyadic estimates of kernel singularities, cancellation, and the Schur test. -> Well-defined mean-zero pairing with tail control
- **step-weak-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weak-compactness): Diagonal weak compactness, support convergence, and passage of pairings with corrected tests. -> Nonzero weak limits inheriting the bounds
- **step-dyadic-lattice** [claim](#stmt-prop-dyadic-lattice) / [proof](#stmt-prop-dyadic-lattice): Regular dyadic cells on an AD-regular set, adjusted in the bounded case. -> Nested cells with comparable masses
- **step-bessel** [claim](#stmt-lem-dyadic-test-bessel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dyadic-test-bessel): Almost-orthogonality of normalized tests and a Schur-type bound for the Gram matrix. -> Bessel inequality for test families
- **step-oscillation-packing** [claim](#stmt-prop-oscillation-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-oscillation-packing): Localization to a fixed outer ball, then the Bessel estimate with the hard-truncation bound. -> Carleson family of oscillation-bad cells
- **step-annular-alternative** [claim](#stmt-lem-annular-alternative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annular-alternative): Two successive tangents reduce the containing dimension, then compactness gives a uniform radius floor. -> Flat ball or large annulus
- **step-annular-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-annular-average-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annular-average-gap): Decomposition of the input into inner, annular and outer parts with kernel derivative estimates. -> Annulus gives an average gap
- **step-flat-seeds** [claim](#stmt-prop-flat-seeds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annular-average-gap-2): Flat branch gives a seed; otherwise Haar Bessel inequality gives a Carleson estimate. -> Flat descendants outside a Carleson family
- **step-plane-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-plane-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plane-comparison): Choice of well-conditioned support points using a lower-dimensional tube estimate. -> Comparison of fitting planes
- **step-fixed-plane** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-plane-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-plane-moments): Chain of neighbouring planes summed over geometric series. -> One reference plane over a finite horizon
- **step-fractional-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-lem-fractional-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fractional-rigidity): Fourier multiplier with a positive symbol, support at the origin of a tempered distribution, and tail exclusion of high degree. -> Affine rigidity
- **step-planar-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-lem-planar-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-planar-rigidity): Full support by a nearest-point blow-up, bounded density, then the fractional equation for the density. -> Constant density on the plane
- **step-flat-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-flat-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flat-limit): Fixed-plane moments, weak limit, reflectionless limit, then planar rigidity. -> Flat limiting measure
- **step-height-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-energy): Test the small pairing against a centered cutoff height and bound the exterior remainder shell by shell. -> Uniform normalized energy
- **step-moving-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-moving-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moving-compactness): Finite partitions of bounded regions, variance control by energy, diagonal subsequence. -> Limiting heights with convergent moments
- **step-height-equation** [claim](#stmt-prop-height-equation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-height-equation): Pass a normalized pairing to the limit with a mean correction and control the exterior tails. -> Fractional equation for the limit height
- **step-polarization** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-polarization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-polarization): Expand the square and use convergence of the cross term and of the norms. -> Strong convergence to an affine height
- **step-normal-planes** [claim](#stmt-lem-normal-affine-planes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normal-affine-planes): Explicit tilted plane built from the affine height. -> Planes with vanishing normalized distance
- **step-excess-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-normalized-excess-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-normalized-excess-limit): Combine the flat limit, the height equation, rigidity, polarization and the tilted planes. -> Vanishing normalized excess
- **step-excess-propagation** [claim](#stmt-thm-excess-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-excess-propagation): Contradiction against the sequential limit, then rescaling to arbitrary center and scale. -> Uniform excess propagation
- **step-seed-charging** [claim](#stmt-lem-seed-charging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-seed-charging): Pointwise counting of groups of parents along a chain of seeds. -> Mass bound for parents
- **step-beta-packing** [claim](#stmt-prop-beta-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-beta-packing): Iterate propagation along a cube chain from a flat seed, then charge failures with seed charging. -> Carleson failures of bilateral flatness
- **step-bwgl** [claim](#stmt-thm-bwgl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-variation-2): Transfer open-ball estimates to the closed-ball and metric coefficients and use the corona decomposition to obtain Lipschitz images. -> Big pieces of Lipschitz images
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-bwgl): Apply the bilateral criterion and rescale the radius. -> Uniform rectifiability
- **step-variation** [claim](#stmt-cor-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-variation): Reduce to the unbounded-support case, apply the Mas–Tolsa forward estimates and identify principal values. -> Variation bounds and principal values
